BOR CROSS-LAYER PRODUCTION APPLICATION REPORT
============================================

Purpose
-------
This report maps the compiled user-space layers discussed during the audit and
contains symbol-scoped x86-64 Intel-syntax assembly from one unified stripped
Bor/geth release executable. It is a focused dump of the boundary functions, not a raw
disassembly of every third-party dependency.

Build
-----
Executable: /tmp/bor-gdb-c72e248.12rk9V/geth.layers.release
Source snapshot: c72e248587a19b1c93eda95871c46b53071e58fd
Build command: go build -ldflags='-s -w' -o geth.layers.release ./cmd/geth
Symbol map: /tmp/bor-gdb-c72e248.12rk9V/geth.layers.prod (same optimized code, retained symbols only for range mapping)
Architecture: ELF 64-bit LSB executable, x86-64, version 1 (SYSV), dynamically linked, interpreter /lib64/ld-linux-x86-64.so.2, BuildID[sha1]=fd4b21601bf39067c8b7d2631c1b4be2119cb770, for GNU/Linux 3.2.0, stripped
SHA-256: 2616b5a03c3ecd12544ea56eabedc7272bf80fe88b6c97f601ffaf044b28ea92
Generated UTC: 2026-11-22T16:18:46Z

Important boundary
------------------
The executable contains user-space code only. Linux kernel implementations of
accept4, bind, connect, read, write, mmap, mprotect, clone3, epoll and related
syscalls are not inside this ELF. This report includes the Go runtime and
internal/poll call sites that transition into those kernel boundaries, plus the
observed strace results below.

Layered execution map
---------------------
1. Network/RPC boundary:
   socket -> bind/listen -> accept4 -> epoll -> read/write
   -> node.(*Node).startRPC
   -> node.(*httpServer).start / node.(*httpServer).ServeHTTP
   -> rpc.(*Server).ServeHTTP / ServeCodec
   -> rpc.(*handler).handleMsg / handleCall / handleBatch
   -> registered API method.

2. Transaction and state boundary:
   API method -> transaction/message validation
   -> stateful.ApplyMessage or transaction processor
   -> ParallelStateDB reads/writes
   -> ValidateDetailed / category validation
   -> serial settlement through StateDB.ApplyMVWriteSet.

3. EVM boundary:
   EVM.Call / StaticCall / DelegateCall / Create / Create2
   -> EVM.Run -> opcode dispatch (runSwitch)
   -> account/storage reads and balance/selfdestruct effects.

4. Process/runtime/memory boundary:
   Go runtime stack growth, goroutine scheduling, mmap/mprotect, poller and
   file descriptor operations. These are user-space transitions before the
   kernel executes the syscall.

5. Kernel boundary:
   The syscall number and arguments leave the process; kernel networking,
   virtual memory, scheduler and file-descriptor implementations are outside
   the geth ELF and require strace/bpftrace/perf or kernel tracing.

Runtime evidence captured separately
-------------------------------------
State fixture: /tmp/bor-strace-v2.fCBKnL
  TestV2BalanceValidation passed. No network syscalls occurred. The trace
  showed mmap/mprotect, clone3, epoll, signal setup and /proc inspection.
RPC fixture: /tmp/bor-rpc-strace.dm17Qf
  Selected node RPC tests passed. The trace showed loopback socket, bind,
  listen, connect, accept4, setsockopt, epoll and cleanup. EADDRINUSE was
  observed only on the intentional fixed-port failure/recovery test.
GDB runtime evidence:
  /tmp/bor-gdb-c72e248.12rk9V/gdb-validate-runtime.dump
  /tmp/bor-gdb-c72e248.12rk9V/gdb-settlement-runtime.dump

-------------------

Assembly notation
-----------------
objdump -d -Mintel; addresses are virtual addresses in the stripped release
executable. The normal optimized production code path is used, with inlining
and dead-code removal enabled.

Scope note
----------
A literal unfiltered objdump of the entire release ELF includes every dependency
and compiler-generated function and is intentionally not included.
The assembly below includes all selected symbols at the discussed layer
boundaries, while excluding unrelated third-party implementation noise.
The production optimizer inlined/eliminated Node.getAPIs; its release path is
represented by the surrounding Node.startRPC and RegisterApis ranges.
The small runtime.mprotect wrapper is likewise absent as a standalone symbol
in this release; runtime.mmap and the runtime syscall-transition ranges remain.
